# SF-H5: When are chip and export controls a security measure, and when a permanent advantage?

Area H · As of clef 7924 Berichte · Luna 7924 Berichte · d3-ultra 4771 Berichte · JEV 5667 Berichte · amos 7924 Berichte · drex 6984 Berichte · SubQ 7924 Berichte

## Subquestions

- [SF-H5-T01](../../fragen/SF-H5/T01/): Will states publish, for each restriction on advanced chips or equipment, the security reason, the duration and the review under which it can be lifted?
- [SF-H5-T02](../../fragen/SF-H5/T02/): Do one state's technology measures and the affected state's objections refer to the same lists, such as entity lists of restricted companies and persons, and export controls? This was asked about China's objections to monopoly and hegemony and the US measures Dario Amodei (Anthropic) advocates in his essay “We Must Pace the Frontier”: chip and equipment bans, action against unauthorised distillation (training one's own model on the outputs of other models) and protection of model weights (the learned parameters).
- [SF-H5-T03](../../fragen/SF-H5/T03/): What observable response would show that technology restrictions are making a verifiable safety agreement less likely while widening the capability lead, and what change in strategy would follow?

## Reports by share of levels 4–7

Levels 4–7 / 3–7 = probability each model gives to these levels (3 = useful background or context, 4 = contains some facts that could be cited as evidence … 7 = directly and substantially answers the question). Listed: reports where clef or Luna give levels 3–7 more than 50 %. Sorted by the higher of clef and Luna for levels 4–7. Further models (JEV, amos, d3 …) rated part of the reports; the others show “not yet rated”. The full distribution over the 8 levels is in the data file.
